## 90. Rev 27: the Port opens, with faction messages and the weekly leaderboard (2026-10-27, awaiting in-editor approval)

**Asked (Shombith):** "Use the design board and lets open up the port now. Right now the port will only contain one tab
called faction messages. Factions will be like different clans but thats in the future currently all players are in a
single faction. So lets make a chatting system for the port and then open up port. Create some mockup designs on the
design board." Then: "The weekly leaderboard can also go to the port for now"; "use the sea line background like
everywhere"; on the chat service, "should we do vivox? We can" and "Lets implement".

**On the canvas:** page "Rev 27 - Port" of Menu Redesign B (https://claude.ai/artifact/W8DBZaW6pu3L28egCjrKo2), with a
copy in `Docs/MenuRedesignB/board/R27*.dc.html`. Five boards, all over the sea lines:
- `R27PortMessages`: interactive. Type, press Enter or SEND, and wait out the 3 s cooldown.
- `R27PortLeaderboard`: the weekly board as the second tab.
- `R27PortActions`: a message's actions (report, mute), the muted list and the report toast.
- `R27PortStates`: the empty, tuning-in, offline and not-sent states.
- `R27Notes`: notes and the open decisions.

**The chat service: Unity Vivox** (decided 2026-10-27). Vivox is UGS: it signs in with the same Authentication player
id, sits in the same dashboard project, and the Moderation package already integrates with it (a report carries the
shared channel). Messages are pushed, Vivox keeps channel history, and it runs on PC, iOS and Android. A clan later is
another channel, and voice can come later on the same SDK. A Cloud Code poller was built first and dropped before
commit. It added no package, but it was not real time and cost a Cloud Code call per poll. The new package is
`com.unity.services.vivox` 16.12.1.

**What the player gets:** PORT, the top bar's last tab, opens. Its lock is lifted, so LT / RT reach it. It has two tabs,
stepped with LB / RB.
- **FACTION MESSAGES.**
  - On the left: the faction card ("All Pilots"; every player is in one faction for now), the three rules, the muted
    pilots with UNMUTE ALL, and the controls.
  - On the right: the feed (the channel's last 50 messages from Vivox history, then every new one pushed live, newest
    at the foot), the field with its count, SEND and the hint line.
  - Enter, X or SEND sends. A message can hold 140 characters, and a player can send one message every 3 s.
  - A refused message stays in the field, with the reason under it in Danger.
  - A or Y on a message (or a click) opens REPORT MESSAGE, MUTE PILOT and CANCEL:
    - Report goes to UGS Moderation as verbal abuse.
    - Mute is kept on this device only.
  - Your own messages carry a neutral bar and YOU, never the selection blue (`Docs/UI_COLOR_RULES.md`). Replaced by the
    sea-lit cards of section 91 (Rev 32 D).
- **WEEKLY LEADERBOARD:** the weekly challenge window's own board (World / Regional / Friends, podium, rows, your best
  run, rewards). It has no BACK, and this week's title and countdown sit at the tab row's right. The configure card's
  LEADERBOARD button still opens the window, unchanged.
- **Safety defaults** (proposed; decision 2 in the notes):
  - A player whose recorded birth year says they may be under 13 can read but not send (`FairPlayAge.BirthYear`;
    an unknown year can send).
  - Message text is never rich text.
  - Control characters and line breaks are removed before sending.

**Built:**
- **C#, the chat** (`Assets/_Scripts/System/FactionChat/`):
  - `FactionChat.cs` holds the pure rules, the feed's log (each message once, in time order), the device mutes and
    the metadata that carries the sender's avatar and name.
  - `FactionChatService.cs` is the Vivox transport: it initialises and logs in once, joins the text-only channel
    `faction_<id>` while the tab is shown and leaves when it is hidden, reads the history and sends.
  - `FactionChatTests` covers the rules and the log: 10 tests, run headless with
    `Tools/Build/prisma_edit_mode_tests/run.sh`.
- **C#, the screen** (`Assets/_Scripts/UI/Elements/Port/`): `PortScreen` (the `IScreen`, two tabs), `FactionMessagesPane`,
  `FactionMessageRow` and `PortLeaderboardPane`. The leaderboard pane drives the same `WeeklyChallengeLeaderboardPanel`
  the window does. Every word is an authored field.
- **ScreenSwitcher:**
  - `disabledScreens` defaults to empty.
  - The PlayFab-era `LeaderboardMenu` field and its `LoadView` on PORT are gone.
  - The old `PortScreen` panel stays in Menu_Main, switched off; deleting it is a cleanup of its own.
- **PlayerReports:** gains `OffensiveMessage = 3`, mapped to UGS Moderation's `VerbalAbuse`.
- **Generator** `Tools/Build/author_menu_port.py`:
  - It writes `FactionMessageRow.prefab` and `PortView.prefab` (Main Menu Screens/Port), places one PortView in the
    old panel's place under `Screens`, and points `ScreenSwitcher.screens`' PORT slot (id 3) at it.
  - `--check` and `--self-test` run 11 plants: the slot left on the old panel, a tab dropped, SEND unwired, rich text
    on, your own row in the selection blue, the field opening the keyboard on focus, the actions shown at rest, the
    board's BACK header on, no sea, the old panel on, and the rules loosened.
- **Top bar:** `author_menu_topnav.py` sets `LOCKED = set()`, which removes PORT's lock view and empties
  `disabledScreens`. `author_elemental_converter.py`'s spent one-shot now only asks that ARK is open.
- **Amoebius:** `Port/src/CosmicShore.Engine/Services/VivoxSdk.cs` stands in for Vivox, like `ModerationSdk.cs`
  (its own `feat(port)` commit), so the engine still compiles the game. In Amoebius the feed reads Offline.

**Two generators, one parent:** the Codex and the Port both insert under `Screens`. The Codex takes the first child and
writes its documents right after Screens'. The Port therefore takes the old panel's place and anchors its documents
after the old panel's transform, so neither re-run moves the other (`.claude/skills/ui-redesign-tracker` T12).

**How it was checked:**
- Every `author_menu_*.py --check` (all 22 green); the Port's and the top bar's `--self-test`.
- `gates.sh`: ALL OK.
- The reference compile: 0 errors in project code, with Vivox 16.12.1's real source compiled in (89 assemblies).
- `dotnet build Port/src/CosmicShore.Live`: 0 errors.
- `FactionChatTests`: 10/10, with a negative control (a loosened age rule fails its test).
- `check_ui_colors.py`.
- Not opened in the Unity editor, and no message has been sent through a live Vivox channel: Vivox must first be
  switched on for the project (`Tools/UGS/README.md`, "Switching on faction messages (Vivox)"). The hand test is
  `Docs/UNITY_VERIFICATION_CHECKLIST.md` "The Port".

**Open decisions (R27Notes):**
1. The faction's name: "All Pilots" is a placeholder.
2. Under-13 accounts: read-only (built), or no Port chat at all.
3. A standard Vivox text channel seats about 200 at once. Past that, the faction needs large text channels (an
   enterprise feature, through Vivox) or rooms.
4. There is no word filter: reports and mutes are the tools for now.

## 92. The Ark's tabs: Squads and Decor beside the converter (2026-10-27, awaiting in-editor approval)

> **The Squads page below is superseded by section 95 (the deck)**: the three cards with CHANGE buttons and PLAY
> Arcade / Arena / Mission are gone. The tab row, Decor, the data and the joining described here still stand.

Shombith (2026-10-27): "Ark - add two more tabs along with the converter. 1. Squads - Users can make / join up to 4
squads and they can use the squad to go into a mission or arena or arcade as they want. Inside the ark they will see
the squad members with their selected vessel, element and pilot and artifact for each squad mate. 2. Decor - Its just
a cosmetic for the ark's banner and external internal skin but lock it we do not need to open it now."

Built on Shombith's pilots-and-artifacts branch (`claude/pensive-dijkstra-qxoxo0`, merged into this branch): its
`SquadBook` / `SavedSquad` / `SquadSlot` (the saved squads, `SQUAD_DATA`) and `PilotArtifactCatalogSO`. No board was
drawn first; the layout follows the converter's (title band left, panels on the black sea).

**The tab row** (`ArkTabsView` on `ArkView`'s root): CONVERTER, SQUADS, DECOR as `KitTextTab` words centred at the top,
one page shown at a time. The converter's overlay now sits under a `Converter` page with its node keys unchanged, so
every fileID the scene points at is the same. `ElementalConverterView` is still the screen's one `IScreen`: it forwards
enter and exit to the tabs (`arkTabs`, wired in the scene), and while another tab is up it hides the 3D picture and
releases its render target, as it does when the player leaves the screen.

**SQUADS** (`SquadsPage`):
- **The list**: the player's squads, made and joined together, at most four (`SquadBook.MaxSquads`; `Add` refuses a
  fifth, `Normalize` trims a longer record). Pressing a row makes it the active squad, the one PLAY takes. NEW SQUAD
  makes one from the player's last hull and its Hangar pilot and first slotted artifact, with Rogue One open for a
  friend and Rogue Two an AI. JOIN A FRIEND lists the friends' squads with a seat open.
- **The squad**: three cards (Leader, Rogue One, Rogue Two). Each shows who flies the seat (you, the friend, an AI
  wingmate, or "Open for a friend") and four picks with CHANGE: vessel (the Hangar's unlocked roster), element (Charge,
  Mass, Space, Time, as a word), pilot (the vessel's default, then its roster) and artifact (none, then what fits the
  vessel; the player's own seat offers only artifacts they have found). The player changes their own seat in any squad,
  and the AI or open seats of a squad they made. The seat button there steps AI to open and back, or opens a friend's
  seat again (REMOVE). DELETE SQUAD (made) or LEAVE SQUAD (joined) needs a second press within 3 s.
- **PLAY: Arcade / Arena / Mission**: makes the squad active, sets `GameDataSO.selectedVesselClass` to the player's
  seat (the configure window opens on it unless the card remembers its own hull), seats their pilot
  (`PilotLoadoutStore`), sends the party invite to each online friend in the squad who is not already in the party,
  then presses the Home hub's entry for that window (`MenuHubButton.Press`). A window Home refuses is refused here the
  same way, before anything changes: Mission stays locked.
- **Joining, with no server in the middle.** Each player publishes a `SquadPublicRecord` to Cloud Save PUBLIC player
  data (`SQUADS_PUBLIC`, `SquadDirectory`): the squads they made, and a claim (`SquadClaim`: owner, squad id, seat, four
  picks) for each squad they joined. A friend joins by writing a claim into their own record. The owner, when they next
  open the tab, reads their friends' records and seats the first claim on each open seat (`SquadMembership.ReconcileOwned`);
  a seated friend whose record no longer claims the seat has left, and the seat opens again. The joiner reads the
  owner's record back (`RefreshJoined`): Pending until the owner seats them, then Seated, SeatLost or Disbanded. Each
  player writes only their own record. Reads are throttled to one round per 20 s and at most 30 friends; a write
  waits 2 s so a run of changes is one write.
- Every word is a serialized field on `SquadsPage` (templates with tokens: `{count}`, `{owner}`, `{open}`, `{squad}`,
  `{seat}`).

**DECOR**: the banner, the outer hull and the inner hull as three locked tiles. Its tab carries a
`MenuAvailabilityView` set to Locked, so a press gets the sting, the shake and "Not open yet." and the page never
shows. Opening it later is setting that one field to Available.

**Data:**
- `SquadSeat` (Ai = 0, Open = 1, Player = 2) and `SquadSlot.Seat` / `MemberId` / `MemberName`. A slot saved before
  these existed reads as an AI seat.
- `SavedSquad.Id` / `OwnerId` / `OwnerName` / `MySeat`.
- `SquadBook.MaxSquads` / `CanAdd` / `IndexOfId`.
- `SquadPublicRecord`, `SquadClaim`, `SquadMembership` (pure, in `CosmicShore.Data`).
- `SquadDirectory` (Cloud Save public read and write), `UGSKeys.SquadsPublic`.
- `SquadMembershipTests` (9 tests).

**Generator:** `author_menu_ark.py` authors the tab row, both pages and the wiring, and validates them: three tabs,
each a KitTextTab over a CanvasGroup page; SQUADS driven by its page; DECOR locked; every row, card and button field
wired; the vessel list and the pilot catalog referenced; the scene's `arkTabs` and `converterTab`. Three new negative
controls (Decor opened, a squad mate with no pilot button, `arkTabs` unwired); all eight fire.

**How it was checked:**
- `author_menu_ark.py --check` and `--self-test` pass.
- The real-reference compile (`unity_refcompile`, player and editor configs) reports 0 errors in project code.
- The 21 squad tests (`SquadMembershipTests` and the existing `SquadBookTests`) were compiled with Roslyn against
  Newtonsoft and run outside Unity: all pass.
- `check_generated_assets.py`, `check_console_logging.py` and `check_conditional_compilation.py` pass.
- Not opened in the Unity editor. The join flow needs two signed-in accounts that are friends.

**Open:**
- Nothing in a match reads the AI seats' picks yet. The mode's own AI backfill flies them; only the player's own
  vessel and pilot reach the launch.
- The element pick is drawn and saved, but no match reads a pre-match element (`Docs/PILOTS_AND_ARTIFACTS.md` §8 Q1).
- Two friends claiming the same open seat in the same moment: the owner seats the first record read and the other
  reads SeatLost. Closing that race needs a Cloud Code reservation (backend, Shombith).
- `author_pilot_assets.py --check` fails on this branch because the Meerkat (on bleeding-edge since the 10-27 merge)
  has no pilots or artifacts. That is a content gap for the pilots work, not caused by the tabs.

## 95. The deck: Rev 31 boards and the Squads tab rebuilt to them (2026-10-27, awaiting in-editor approval)

**Boards:** page "Rev 31 - Squads, the deck" of Menu Redesign B (Rev 30 was taken by a Codex page the same hour), copies
in `Docs/MenuRedesignB/board/R31Deck*.dc.html`: Make squad (interactive), squad made, a joined squad, no squad yet, join a
friend's squad, Decor locked, notes.

**Built** (`author_menu_ark.py` authors it; `SquadsPage` drives it):
- **List** down the left: four rows (name, domain, "Yours - 1 seat open" / "Kestrel's squad - joined"), MAKE SQUAD and
  JOIN A FRIEND'S SQUAD under them.
- **Header**: the kicker, the squad's name and line, and the domain chips (Jade, Ruby, Gold, each with its diamond in
  the domain's colour).
- **The deck**: three cards (`SquadMateCard`), the leader raised in the middle and the rogues turned 6 degrees on the
  flanks. Each card has the seat and its number, the hull (the Hangar card render, `HangarCardArtSO`), who flies it, and
  four rows: vessel, element, pilot, artifact. The pilot's full figure stands over the card's left edge. An open seat
  shows an outline instead ("A friend takes this seat").
- **Make squad**: the deck scales to 0.8 about its top centre and the picker opens under it (`SquadPickChip`):
  - vessel tiles, seven a page, with < and > to page through the Hangar's unlocked roster;
  - the pilots (base kit and the vessel's roster, with their portraits);
  - the four elements, and for a rogue card who flies it (AI wingmate or open for a friend);
  - the artifacts (none, then the vessel's and the fleet's; the player's own card offers only found ones).

  A press on a card picks which one the picker edits. CREATE SQUAD adds the squad; EDIT reopens a made squad (SAVE
  SQUAD). Nothing is saved before that press, and CANCEL drops the draft.
- **Take into**: Mission (its padlock follows Home's Mission entry) and Arena only, shown only for a made squad. No
  Arcade.
- **Domain**: `SavedSquad.Domain` (a record from before reads as Jade; a joined copy takes the owner's). A press plays
  `SquadNanobotSwap` on each card, 0.14 s apart. The hull dissolves into 34 prism-bots that scatter and rebuild it tinted
  to the new domain (`SO_ColorSet.GetDomainSignalColor`), on unscaled time. Only the leader changes a squad's domain.
- The first cut's three-card editor with CHANGE buttons and its PLAY ARCADE / ARENA / MISSION row are gone.
  `author_menu_ark.py` now fails if a Take-into-Arcade field comes back.

## 96. The squad's domain reaches the launch panel (2026-10-27)

**Asked (Shombith):** "wire the squad domain into the configure window".

The configure window already re-picks a remembered per-card domain once its panel opens
(`ArcadeGameConfigureModal.RestoreRememberedDomain`, sent to the server through `HandleDomainSelected` so the tile is never
lit without the server knowing). A squad going in now wins over that memory:
- **The player who pressed TAKE INTO** (the launch authority): `SquadsPage.Play` sets `SquadLaunchIntent` to a copy of
  the squad. Every card they open on that trip starts on the squad's domain. A press on a Home hub entry clears the
  intent (`MenuHubButton.HandlePlayerClick`; the Ark's own press goes through `Press`, which keeps it). A plain Arena trip
  from Home therefore keeps each card's own memory, even with squad mates in the party.
- **A squad mate who joined that party** (a guest): their panel opens on their active squad's domain when the party holds
  one of its mates (`SquadMembership.PartyIsSquad`), so the squad flies as one team.
- Both are decided by one pure function, `SquadMembership.TryLaunchDomain`, covered by two new tests.

A squad domain that the card's domain count does not reach (Gold on a two-domain card) falls back to Jade, the same
`LaunchPreferenceRules.ResolvePilotDomain` rule a remembered pick goes through. The domain count is never widened for it:
that is a host term, and the window keeps its own rules for it. The player can still press another tile, and the ready
press then remembers that pick for the card, as before. The weekly challenge keeps its pinned domain.

**How it was checked:**
- `unity_refcompile` player and editor configs: 0 errors in project code.
- 24 squad tests, run outside Unity: all pass.
- The console-logging and conditional-compilation gates pass, and `author_menu_ark.py --check` shows no drift.
- Not opened in the Unity editor. Needs a hand test with two friends: Take into Arena from a Ruby squad, open a card,
  and both the host and the invited squad mate start on Ruby.

## 97. The Codex rebuilt around play, the Arcade's two dropdowns, and the first Hangar fixes (2026-10-27, awaiting in-editor approval)

Shombith's review of section 89 (2026-10-27), with the later asks in the same session: "the models of the crystals
are old ... update the whole codex and everything animate ... animate in the descriptions" and "Remove the bobbing
animation and use some cool selection animations".

**The Codex: three chapters, not seven.**
- **ELEMENTS.** Charge, Mass, Space and Time, each with its Etherion crystal as its picture and every hull's ability
  for it. Then three authored cards: **Omni**, **Prisms** and **Spindles**. The Etherions chapter is gone, and so are
  the crystal build notes it showed (triangle counts, heart sizes, "dropped by 22 species").
- **ECOLOGY.** Flora and Fauna follow the Toy Box's Spawn Matrix (`Toy_SpawnMatrix.asset`), so they list every species
  a player can release, in its order: 23 fauna (the swarm colonies, Piranha, Pack Hunter, Leviathan and the rest) and 27
  flora. Before this the Codex listed 6 and 16. Each species' Toy Box description is its gameplay line. Where
  `Codex.asset` has the species (matched ignoring spaces and dashes), its picture, facts, element cells and elemental
  laws fill in the rest. A species only the Codex knows still follows the list.
- **LEXICON.** One card each for Vessels, Pilots, Artifacts and Toys, in place of their drill-downs. The words are
  authored on `CodexScreen.words.lexicon`.
- **Spelling.** The player reads **Etherion** everywhere. The serialized names (`CodexSO.ethirions`,
  `CodexKingdom.Ethirion`, the `ethirion.*` ids and discovery keys) are unchanged, because a rename would break saved
  data and Garrett's harvester.

**The page reads play first.** Kicker and title, then what it does in play, then facts, element cells and rows, then
the lore last, under a LORE heading (`CodexPageView.gameplayText`, `loreBlock`). Each element has a short authored lore
line.

**Motion (no bobbing).** Nothing moves until an entry is picked:
- **The tile.** A bright scan line sweeps down it once (`CodexTile.scan`, 0.42 s).
- **The picture.** It plays its element's entrance (`CodexPortrait`, 0.55 s). Charge jolts in on a crackle. Mass drops
  from 46 units and lands heavy with a squash. Space warps in on a 120° turn from small. Time ticks into place in four
  clock steps. Anything else punches up 12 %.
- **While picked.** A crystal's flipbook plays its real element effect, rendered from the crystal labs (a 4×4 sheet,
  `elementFlipbooks`, at 8 fps). The element badge's petals light one by one.
- **The page.** The gameplay line and the lore type themselves in at 140 characters a second. Each line of facts,
  cells and rows grows in, 50 ms after the one above it.
- **Lifeforms** still drift through the domain colours.

**The crystal pictures.** `Assets/_Graphics/Codex/ethirion_*.png` (also read by the Ark) were baked on 2026-09-25,
before the Omni Motion Lab round 5 crystals graduated. They are re-rendered from the labs, keeping their guids, and
each crystal gains a 16-frame flipbook in `Assets/_Graphics/Codex/Flipbooks/`.

**The Arcade: two dropdowns.**

- **Filters.** ELEMENT (All, Charge to Time) and VESSEL (All, then each hull an arcade card seats, in the Hangar's list
  order: 13 today). The two narrow together. The locked/unlocked and solo/multiplayer filters are gone.
- **The card** shows its genre's one petal, white (`GameCard.GenrePetal`), not the flower filled to a hull's bought

**The Hangar, first pass.**

### 97.3 The build fix, the Lexicon's ways to play, and Rev 30 boards (2026-10-27)

Shombith, with a screenshot of the built Codex: "cant see anything", "In lexicon teach about missions arcades voyages too
quest oo, also hate the UI now, create some new mockups".

- **Cause of "can't see anything".** A tile waits its turn (30 ms per index) before it fades in. One long frame, such as
  the hitch as the screen opens, could carry a waiting tile past the whole fade in a single step. That tile never drew
  its last frame and stayed at alpha 0. Only the first tile, which waits for nothing, showed. `CodexTile.Update` now
  always lands the final step.
- **Lexicon.** It now has Arcade, Quests, Voyages and Missions cards first, the ways to play, then Vessels, Pilots,
  Artifacts and Toys. The art is SkimRace's card, Tandava's card, the Duel for the Cell card and the star.
- **Rev 30 boards** (canvas page `rev30`; R29 was taken by another session's Squads round; copies in
  `Docs/MenuRedesignB/board/`):
  - **A Vault** (interactive): a list on the left, the crystal alive on a stage in the middle (its flipbook and its
    element's entrance), and a solid reading panel on the right.
  - **B Field Guide**: a two-page plate with callouts, and an entry strip under the book.
  - **C Orbit**: the Omni crystal at the core, the four elements orbiting it, ecology and lexicon on the outer ring.
  - **Lexicon** (interactive): image cards and a reading panel.
  - **Every option** puts solid panels under its words; the sea behind the text is what washed the build out. Each shows
    the real abilities from the shipped ability maps.
  - Waiting on a pick before the build changes again.

## 98. The Codex as an Orbit (Rev 30 board C, 2026-10-27, awaiting in-editor approval)

Shombith picked board C: "Go with C, implement it". The grid of cards is gone. Generator: `Tools/Build/author_menu_codex.py`
(`--check`, `--self-test`, 17 planted defects).

- **The orbit (left).** A chapter's entries ride three tilted rings (`CodexOrbit`, on `Orbit/Nodes`):
  - The rings hold 4, 8 and 12 entries, inside first. The outer ring takes any overflow. Their half-widths are 270, 440
    and 600, at a tilt of 0.46. Each ring is a thin circle (`kit_ring_line`) stretched to its ellipse, hidden while
    empty.
  - Depth is how far round to the front an entry is. The front (the bottom of the ellipse) draws at 1.05x and full
    alpha, on top. The back draws at 0.62x and half alpha, underneath.
  - **The core** is the picked entry's picture, 240 square over a breathing selection-blue halo (`kit_ring_glow`). It
    sits among the entries at mid depth, so an entry passing in front covers it and one passing behind is covered. It
    is the page's `CodexPortrait`: it plays the element's entrance and the crystal flipbook (section 97).
  - **Picking an entry** turns its ring the shortest way round until the entry is at the front (0.6 s, OutCubic). Only
    that ring moves, and nothing moves while nothing is turning.
- **The entries.** Each is a round node: a dark disc (`kit_ring_fill`, new), the picture on it, and an outline ring
  that `CodexTile` paints selection blue on the open entry, over a breathing halo. The name and a quiet line sit under
  it. The pick's scan line is gone; it was a card's effect.
- **Pad and keys.** `CodexScreen` wires each entry's navigation explicitly: left and right step to the previous and next
  entry (wrapping), and up goes to the open chapter's tab (FLORA or FAUNA under ECOLOGY). The navigation is explicit
  because the entries move, and automatic navigation would follow where they happen to be drawn. LB / RB still step
  chapters.
- **The page (right).** A solid reading panel, 700 wide, in the selection blue's border and a soft breathing glow. Words
  only: kicker, title, then gameplay, facts, element cells (two columns now) and rows, with lore last. The sea no
  longer shows through the text (section 97.3).
- **New kit sprite:** `kit_ring_fill`, a solid disc (`author_ui_kit_sprites.py`, checked as a quarter-pi of its square).
- **Retired:** the grid's ScrollRect and GridLayoutGroup, and `CodexScreen.gridScroll` / `EnsureVisible`.
- **Not verified in the editor.** Both reference compiles are clean, and the offline render shows the rings, core and
  panel. The orbit's placement is runtime-only, so the offline renderer cannot show the entries on their rings.
  `CodexOrbitTests` covers the ring fill and the picked entry's front position.

### 98.3 Pictures for the GPU-drawn fauna, and Toy Box names in the Codex (2026-10-27)

Shombith, after Scan & Merge and Bake Missing ("Baked 0 image(s)", with every swarm and substrate species reported as
"nothing to photograph"): "None of the fauna got their images".

- **Why nothing was photographed.** The substrate packs (Harrier, Leech, Leviathan, Locust, Lurker, Mobber, Pack
  Hunter, Shoal, Siege, Stampede), the swarms (Bench, Tandava, NCA Lizard) and the builder colonies (Fortress, Thief
  Nest, Wearer) are POPULATIONS. Each root is a heartless, bodiless anchor, and its lifeforms live in a simulation and
  are drawn from one GPU buffer, so the prefab carries no mesh at all.
- **The fix: `CodexImageBaker.BuildPopulation`.** After the prefab's own meshes and the colony chain, the baker finds
  the member the GPU draws, by field name: `SubstrateSpeciesSO.agentPrefab`, `SwarmFaunaConfigSO.TadpolePrefab` or
  `BuilderColonyConfigSO.MemberPrefab`, reached through the root's `species` or `config`. It photographs a loose school
  of nine members on a golden-angle disc, each turned and sized a little differently. Each member wears the same body
  prism every agent wears.
- **Why a picture would not have reached its page anyway.** The harvester named a species after its config assets.
  The substrate ones came out as "Substrate Harrier Fauna Config Data", because the suffix strip did not know
  " Config Data" and only stripped the element before the kingdom. The bench swarms, four Toy Box species on one
  prefab, came out as one entry. The Codex screen lists the Toy Box roster and finds each page by the roster's name,
  so those pages were unreachable.
- **The fix: `CodexHarvester.GroupSpecies`.** Every config the Toy Box's Spawn Matrix lists becomes that roster
  species, under the roster's name ("Harrier", "Swarm Field"). A config the roster does not list joins the roster
  species that uses its prefab (the odd `WormColonyFaunaConfig` stays with the Worm Colony). Anything else groups by
  prefab, as before. The suffix strip now also drops " Config Data" / " Config" and takes the element and kingdom in
  either order.
- **Before re-running:** discard the local `Assets/Resources/Codex.asset` changes from the first scan. Those entries
  carry the config-asset names, and the merge never renames an entry, so they would linger beside the new ones as
  extra Field Guide entries.

### 98.4 Vessel Changer pictures: the hull, not the skimmer (2026-10-27)

Shombith: "fix the vessel changer pictures too". Twelve `tool_vessel-changer__<hull>.png` came out byte-identical:
Angler, Brittlestar, Dolphin, Goby, Grizzly, Manta, Meerkat, Squirrel, Starling, Stoat, Thresher and Urchin.

- **Cause.** Every ship prefab carries a skimmer: a builtin sphere scaled 15-60x. The Codex baker photographed the whole
  prefab, so the sphere filled the frame, and after normalising every hull was the same blue ball. The Hangar stage and
  the Toy Box never had this, because they build through `VesselModelBuilder`, whose hull filter drops builtin
  primitives, trails, jets and VFX.
- **Fix.** `CodexImageBaker.HarvestModel` takes an optional part filter, and `CodexVariantSubject.BuildHull` passes
  `VesselModelBuilder.IsHull`, now public: one hull filter for every picture of a ship.
- **To re-bake:** FrogletTools > UI > Codex, then **Bake All**. Bake Missing skips these, because a file already exists.

### 98.5 The Ark's sea, a Blue-only Codex, and the Hangar's hull as the ship with silver plates (2026-10-27)

Shombith, with a screenshot of the Ark's Squads tab (the game world showing straight through): "1. Ark no sea line
2. All codex should have blue domain not anything else 3. Hangar vessels should not look like this it should look like
normal vessel but just the domain parts should look silver".

- **Ark.** The Squads and Decor pages now open on the window backdrop every style-B window draws: the near-opaque veil
  under the top bar, then the sea lines (`Sea_Window.mat`). The pair is `author_menu_ark.page_backdrop`, first under
  each page, so the page's CanvasGroup hides it with the page. The converter keeps its 3D picture and draws none. The
  generator asserts the pair on both pages and has a new planted defect, "the Squads tab without its sea lines".
- **Codex: Blue only.** Pictures are tinted from `CodexScreen.pictureDomains`, which is `{ Blue }`, instead of drifting
  through Jade, Ruby and Gold. Blue is nobody's team, and an encyclopedia page belongs to no one. The Etherion crystals
  keep their rendered colour: a crystal is not a domain's.
- **Hangar: the ship, with silver plates.** §98.2's whole-hull repaint is withdrawn:
  - `HangarVesselStage.silverSwaps` and `WearSilver` are removed.
  - The `SilverBody` / `SilverWindow` materials are deleted.
  - The body, windows and engines wear the ship's own materials again.
  - Only the domain-role slots change (`VesselModelBuilder.TryBuildLive`, as before §98.2). They wear
    `ClassLiveryVesselMaterial`, now a neutral silver: rim 0.96 / 0.97 / 1, face 0.60 / 0.63 / 0.70.

## 99. Rev 33: into a game mode, and why it lags (2026-10-28; Shombith picked A, built in 99.4)

Shombith: "Next up is the intro to game modes, I hate the old UI, create mockups maybe look it like when we swap cells,
it lags when we get into a game mode, make it look a bit AAA". Published on the canvas, page `rev33` (version
`1793157683-c2fa`), and copied to `Docs/MenuRedesignB/board/`. Each board plays the whole entry on a loop. The BOARD
buttons at its top-left pause it on each phase.

- **A Cell gate (recommended).** The cell-change screen (`EnvironmentLoadVeil`, section 47.4) grown into the mode
  entry. The Omni crystal in its hex fills with the build. ENTERING sits over the mode's title, tagline, objective and
  intensity. The four real steps tick, the pilots' READY pips turn lime, and the tip and slim bar sit below. At 100% the
  crystal flashes, its hex becomes a window onto the arena, and then it bursts into the intro's letterbox. The
  countdown fills the hex a third per beat; GO is lime. Shown with Skim Race.
- **B Lineup.** Regatta's six seats as tall pilot cards in three domain groups. A card lights up when its pilot
  finishes loading. At 100% the lineup takes one impact and the outer domains turn to face the middle. The art pushes
  into the title, and a crystal counts down.
- **C Briefing.** Dog Fight in the Boneyard as a calm briefing:
  - the arena map, framed at the size its render holds up at, develops under a scan line as the cell builds;
  - the objective, and the tagline's two numbers (GUNS 1, MISSILES 50);
  - your Sparrow and pilot, both squads with ready pips;
  - a launch bar that turns READY.
  Then the map grows into the intro.
- **D Intro and countdown.** The part every loading screen hands into (today's `IntroCameraOverlay` and the
  Ready / waiting / countdown HUD), restyled:
  - a backlit title slam, the objective tag, and a pilot card (hull, pilot, player);
  - hold to skip, with a hex ring that fills while held;
  - the "Waiting for pilots" card;
  - a crystal that fills on 3-2-1 and breaks open on GO.
  Every element maps to an existing overlay slot. The new authored fields it needs are listed on the board's notes:
  the Entering kicker, the title sweep, the intensity template, the pilot line, the skip hold time and the countdown
  crystal.
- **Shared rules.** Elements carry no colour. Lime appears only for ready and GO, blue only for you, and domain
  colours only for teams. The card art is only ever shown blurred, or framed at its native size.
- **Open points from the art-director passes:**
  - The Omni crystal render is close to the ready lime.
  - Squirrel has two pilot portraits, so a three-seat lineup repeats one.
  - The intro is shown over the blurred card art, standing in for the real intro camera.
  - D's letterbox is 120 px, against the shipped 0.085 of the screen height.

### 99.1 What was measured

The 28 "Game launch" reports Load Time Insights recorded on its own and sent to the `bugledger/*` branches (Windows
Editor, 2026-10-25 to 27, 11 modes; a build is faster):

- **START to the arena ready (the connecting screen done):** 8.9 s typical, 12.3 s at p90, 18.8 s slowest (Cleave).
  About 4.1 s of that is behind the splash (the scene load is about 2.5 s); the connecting screen is up about 4 s.
- **Every launch froze twice right after the connecting screen appeared.** Two back-to-back frames of 1.2 to 1.9 s,
  2.6 s together typically, up to 5.7 s. Both start within about 50 ms of the "Connecting panel shown" mark. The span
  names the reports give these frames (the InitDelayMs gate, preSpawnDelayMs, a prism lay) are just whatever span
  happened to be open.
- **15 of 28 sat out the screen's floor** (about 3.5 to 4 s) with nothing left to build; heavy arenas run 6 to 10 s.
- **About 38 frames a second typical while loading**, 23 at the low end.

### 99.2 Why, from the code

Three readers traced the path. A skeptic re-checked every claim against the code, and these are the ones that held.

- **Two more cameras at the freeze.** `ConnectingPanelController.ShowAsync` turns on the panel's own camera and
  `ConnectingArenaPreview.Begin` creates the preview camera and its render texture. Both render the arena with shadows
  (`ConnectingPanelController.cs:266`, `:281-282`), in the same continuation as the "shown" mark
  (`MiniGameHUD.cs:663-667`). The panel camera draws behind the panel's opaque, full-screen image, so nothing it draws
  is seen. Shader compiles are likely part of the freeze:
  - the editor compiles shaders synchronously (`EditorSettings.asset` `m_AsyncShaderCompilation: 0`);
  - the warm-up collection is empty (`GameplayShaderWarmup.shadervariants` `m_Shaders: {}`).
  This is the most likely cause of the measured freeze, but it is not proven without a profiler capture.
- **The gameplay camera is never rested.** The preview mutes `Camera.main` (`ConnectingArenaPreview.cs:500`), but the
  live player camera, `CM PlayerCam`, is Untagged (`Bootstrap.unity:763-764`). Up to three cameras draw the arena every
  frame of the hold.
- **The watched build tempo.** The panel slices the build at 25 ms (lay and integration) plus 18 ms (creation) a
  frame (`ConnectingPanel.prefab:126-127`, `ConnectingPanelController.cs:269-272`). That is under about 20 fps while
  laying, and slower overall than the covered 80 ms of the cell-change screen. The preview's 20 Hz cap does nothing
  below 20 fps (`ConnectingArenaPreview.cs:278-283`).
- **The grow-in is waited out.** Only `EnvironmentLoadVeil` sets `PrismTrailBuilder.LoadGateCovered`
  (`EnvironmentLoadVeil.cs:431`), so the game's hold never snaps the bloom (`PrismTrailBuilder.cs:420`). That is about
  3 to 4 s on laid arenas; the measured "Arena settle" has a median of 3.2 s.
- **Floors and fixed waits:**
  - `dwellSeconds: 4` in `ConnectingPanel.prefab:132` (the code default and its comments say 2);
  - `waitBeforeLoading` 0.5 s (`SceneLoader.cs:34`);
  - `InitDelayMs` 1 s (`MultiplayerMiniGameControllerBase.cs:23`);
  - the cell's 0.75 s settle before it builds (`Cell.cs:2199`).
- **At the intro.** One frame runs all of these (`MiniGameHUD.cs:699-717`):
  - a `FindObjectsByType<MeshRenderer>` scan of every prism (`IntroCameraScene.cs:141`);
  - a `Resources.Load` of the intro library (`PreGameCinematicController.cs:126`);
  - building the overlay's 9 GameObjects and 5 texts (`IntroCameraOverlay.cs`).
  The intro clock adds raw `Time.deltaTime` (`PreGameCinematicController.cs:287`), so that hitch shows as a camera jump.
- **Also:**
  - two full `GC.Collect` calls per host entry (`SceneLoader.cs:533`, `:303`);
  - online, the slowest peer holds everyone, for up to 45 s (`ConnectingPanel.prefab:129`).

### 99.3 What the new screens change, and next steps

- **A, B and C all cover the arena while it builds.** Built that way, the preview and the panel camera go. The build
  can run at the veil's tempo and set `LoadGateCovered`, which skips the grow-in. Estimates, to be measured:
  - about 3 s off laid arenas;
  - very likely the 2.6 s freeze, too.
- **A floor of about 1 s instead of 4 s** takes up to 3 s off every light mode.
- **D prepares the intro during the load:**
  - take the landmarks from the spawners instead of a scene scan;
  - hold the library by reference;
  - build the overlay once and reuse it;
  - clamp the intro clock.
  Recording the shader warm-up collection takes the first-use compiles off the reveal.
- **Measuring.** Load Time Insights stops when the connecting screen ends. Adding spans for the panel's first frame,
  the dwell and the peer wait would name the freeze in the next recordings, which arrive on their own.
- **Status.** Shombith picked A and asked for the lag fixes (99.4). The areas they touch:
  - the connecting screen and load path are mostly Yash's;
  - the prism build (`PrismTrailBuilder`, used through its load-gate API, not changed) and the cell's build timing
    are Garrett's;
  - the intro cameras (#1582) are Shombith's.

### 99.4 Built: A, the Cell gate, and the lag fixes (2026-10-28)

Shombith: "Implement a. Also Fix the loading lag". Not opened in the editor. Compiled against the Unity reference
assemblies (player and editor), and the generators check and self-test clean.

**The loading screen** (`ConnectingPanel.prefab`, generated by `author_ingame_hud.py` section 12c, every object
re-derived each run):
- It **covers the arena**. The panel's own camera and the arena preview (`ConnectingArenaPreview`, kept in the
  tree, no longer used here) are gone. The backdrop is two `HexApertureGraphic` layers: a solid base and the sea
  (`Sea_Window.mat`). Each is a full-screen cover with a hex hole that starts at radius 0, so at rest nothing shows
  through. The panel's own Image is transparent and still blocks input.
- The **Omni crystal** sits in a `LoadCrystalGraphic` hex at the board's place: glass, spokes and outline, the Omni
  render faint behind, the same render filled from the bottom, and a level line. The crystal's level follows the
  cell-change screen's own stage math (`EnvironmentLoadVeil.StepFor` / `CrystalLevel`).
- **The brief.** ENTERING (the veil's word, between two hairlines), then:
  - the mode's title: Aldrich 124, auto-sized down to 48;
  - the tagline and objective: the intro library's words for the mode;
  - the intensity chip with four pips.
- **The steps panel** (left). Its header is the phase: Connecting to shore, Building the arena, Settling the arena,
  Waiting for pilots. Below are the four real steps (the veil's words). Each step has:
  - a hex tick that fills while its step runs;
  - a check mark once it is done;
  - an underline that grows across the step.
  The panel ends with the `{done} / {total} prisms` count.
- **The pilots panel** (right) is `ConnectingPlayerRoster` in a new **row mode**:
  - one row per pilot, AI included: a domain stripe, the avatar, the name with a YOU (Selection) or AI badge, the
    hull's name, and a pip and Loading / Ready in lime;
  - the `{ready} / {total} Ready` count in the header;
  - the panel grows past the board's 340 for the modes that seat up to 12.
  The old AvatarDomain chip is kept as the row template, so GameCanvas's four overrides into it still land.
- **The footer**: TIP and one of the mode card's own tips, LOADING, the slim bar (the existing Slider) and its
  percentage (`{pct}%`).
- **The gate** (`ConnectingGateView.Open`, 1.7 s):
  - the brief and the panels fade;
  - the crystal charges to white and flashes;
  - the hole opens as the crystal's hex, glides to the centre at radius 452, and bursts past the screen, with a white
    ring on its edge.
  The panel stops blocking input as the gate starts. The intro starts underneath it, so its bars come in through the
  hole.

**The intro** (`IntroCameraOverlay`, the look in `IntroCameraLibrary.asset` via `author_intro_cameras.py`):
- 132-unit bars (0.1222 of the frame) that ease in over 0.8 s instead of popping on (`barsInSeconds`).
- The title (Aldrich 118, rising out of its own mask), the tagline (Chakra 26) and the objective in a framed chip sit
  on a column 128 units in from the left, low on the frame.
- The lower third is a framed panel at the same margin on the right, with a bar in the pilot's colour.
- The library gained `detailFont` (Chakra Petch SemiBold); `captionFont` is Aldrich.
- Skip is unchanged: a press, not D's hold. Hold to skip is not built.

**The countdown** (`CountdownTimer`, the text path):
- The count sits in a 124-radius hex (`beatHex`) that fills from the bottom through each number.
- At GO the hex bursts outward and a lime ring leaves it (`goRing`). The numbers are 168 and GO is 132
  (`numberSize`, `goSize`).
- Found on the way: the beat text auto-sized, so the authored 260 was capped at the Display role's 48. Auto-size is
  now off, and the generator fails if it comes back.

**The lag fixes** (99.2's causes, in order):
- **No camera renders the world under the screen.** The panel and preview cameras are gone. The gameplay camera
  (`CustomCameraController.Camera`, Untagged, so `Camera.main` never found it) is parked with `PilotCameraPark` and
  released when the gate opens.
- **The covered tempo.** Laying runs at the cell-change screen's 80 ms slice, with 512 creation completions a frame
  (`coveredLayBudgetMs`, `coveredCreationBudgetMs`). `LoadGateCovered` is set, so the build skips the grow-in.
- **A 1 s floor** (`dwellSeconds`, was 4). The race the old floor hid is closed:
  - `MultiplayerMiniGameControllerBase.InitializeAfterDelay` now brackets the InitDelayMs wait and
    `InitializeGame` with `PrismTrailBuilder.BeginArenaBuild` / `EndArenaBuild`, so the arena-ready poll cannot pass
    before the build starts;
  - the cell skips its 0.75 s settle while the load gate holds.
- **One GC on the way in.** `SceneLoader` skips the after-load collect when it collected before the load. A return
  to the menu keeps both. `waitBeforeLoading` is 0.1 s (was 0.5).
- **The intro is prepared during the hold** (`PreGameCinematicController.Prepare`, called by `MiniGameHUD` the first
  time the arena-ready poll passes). It loads the library, picks the intro, gathers the arena and builds the overlay
  once, then reuses it.
  - Crystals come from `Crystal.Active`.
  - The landmark scan runs only for an intro that uses landmarks.
  - The intro clock is clamped to 1/30 s a step (`maxClockStep`), so a hitch slows the camera instead of jumping it.

**Not done, still open:**
- The shader warm-up collection is still empty. It needs the editor (record the variants after a play of each mode).
- Load Time Insights spans for the panel's first frame, the dwell and the peer wait.
- ETA text.
- Hold to skip.
- Measured before/after numbers. The next Load Time Insights reports arrive on their own.

**Hand test** (`Docs/UNITY_VERIFICATION_CHECKLIST.md`, "Rev 33 A"): launch Skim Race, a heavy arena (Cleave) and a
6-seat mode. Watch:
- the screen covers from the first frame;
- the steps tick and the pilots turn Ready;
- the gate opens into the intro;
- the hex countdown runs;
- the freeze after the screen appears.

## 100. Rev 34 mockups: FTUE, the captain guides from the side (2026-10-28, rejected; redrawn as Rev 36, section 102)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-10-28):** "Create FTUE mpckups - look at the old desing the captain/pilot shouldcome from the
side and guidethe players".

**The old design (Rev 21, `Docs/MenuRedesignB/board/R21*.dc.html`).** The same six steps (Home, Arcade, Configure, the Lesson, the first race, the first
finish) were guided by a caption strip under the lit control. No one spoke: the guide, the Lesson and the Mentor were text.

**On the canvas:** page "Rev 34 - FTUE: the captain guides from the side"; the copies are `Docs/MenuRedesignB/board/R34*.dc.html`.
Each option board steps through all six steps (CSS-only, radio tabs). In every option the captain of the first hull (Trace,
the Squirrel's pilot) enters from the side AWAY from the lit control. That is left for Home, Arcade, the race and the finish,
and right for Configure and the Lesson. She says one line and leaves when the player presses. The LOCKED guided-path rule
holds: one control lit in lime, everything else dimmed and dead except Settings, and the player presses it.
- **R34A, Wingmate (recommended):** her portrait and a line plate slide in from the side. A dashed lime leader runs from
  the plate to the lit control, and the plate moves off the control when they would overlap.
- **R34B, Comm link:** a compact transmission card docks mid-height on the far side. It has the smallest footprint and
  reads like a radio call, so it also works in the match.
- **R34C, Briefing:** bars come in, a bust rises in the lower corner, and her line runs wide along the bottom. The most
  cinematic; the bottom bar is a gradient so the hub buttons show through.
- **R34Compare:** the three entrances side by side on one step. **R34Notes:** the asks, the side rule and the build notes.

**Rejected (Shombith, 2026-10-28):** "for the ftue I do not like them once bit. Up your game bhai", then "Leave that
ref, just make it good". Section 102 is the redraw.

**Why A:** it says most clearly that a pilot is here with you, and the leader line ties her words to the control without a
second spotlight. B is the fallback for in-match steps, which could use B even if A is picked for the menu.

**Differs from the build:**
- The words move into the guide asset (MainQuest / the Lesson seed), one line per step.
- The captain comes from the hull's pilot roster, using a portrait the roster already has.
- The dialogue system (`DialogueSpeaker`, `MainMenuDialogueView`) already carries the speaker and the line; the side and
  the entrance are new view work.
- Open (Shombith, Caleb): the captain's name and voice, and whether the Mentor's preview tips become hers.

### 100.1 The arcade cards draw the element shapes (2026-10-28)

Shombith: "the arcade cards again are using the old elelmental icons again". The card's genre image drew the lone HUD
petal (`ElementPetals/*_petal.png`, one of the flower's five), which reads as the old diamond icon set. It now draws the
element's SHAPE (pentagon, triangle, kite, diamond from `Assets/_Graphics/ElementShapes`), the icon the Codex and the ability
rows use (section 89). It stays white, 24 px, centred in its 34 px slot. `GameCard.GenreShapes` holds the four (Charge to
Time; an empty slot falls back to the petal), `author_menu_arcade.py` writes them, and the validation fails when they are
not the four shapes ("the genre icon back to the petal" plant).

### 100.2 Connection lost, in style B (2026-10-28)

Shombith: "complete the leftover popups like connection lost". `DisconnectNotice`, the notice that appears in every scene
when the device goes offline, was the last popup still built in code in the old flat look. It now instantiates
`Assets/_Prefabs/UI Elements/Popups/DisconnectNoticeView.prefab` (written by `Tools/Build/author_disconnect_notice.py`,
`--check` / `--self-test`):
- The window looks like the boot Retry window: a slivered window with corner brackets, the "Connection" eyebrow, the
  title and the line, over a 0.82 dim.
- RECONNECT is the lime Play button with an A keycap. It is selected when the notice opens, so A arrives as a submit.
- CONTINUE OFFLINE is grey with a B keycap. It answers the pad's B, but only while the window's group is live.
- Every word still comes from `DisconnectNoticeConfig.asset`, which now names the prefab in `WindowPrefab`. If that field
  is empty, the notice falls back to the old code-built panel.
- Known limit: while freestyle hands the pad to the vessel (`sendNavigationEvents` off), the pad cannot press either
  button. Mouse and touch still can.

Other leftover popups:
- The boot Retry window was already in style B.
- `PopupPanel.prefab` and `ReconnectButton.cs` are unused.
- The freestyle `Pause_Menu_Panel` and the quest `DialogueSetUI` are still the legacy look. The dialogue panel waits on the
  Rev 34 pick (the captain's line is its replacement).

## 101. Rev 35 mockups: hover, how the menu answers the pointer (2026-10-28; Shombith picked C, built in 101.1)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-10-28):** "Then we need some new hover animations".

**Today:**
- `KitButton` lightens its fill 8 % and its border 10 % on hover.
- `KitHoverTint` lifts a word to TextLight (nav tabs, keycaps).
- Tiles and cards have no hover at all, and nothing moves, so the pointer gets almost no answer.

**On the canvas:** page "Rev 35 - Hover: how the menu answers the pointer"; the copies are
`Docs/MenuRedesignB/board/R35*.dc.html`. The four option boards show the same Arcade screen: nav tabs, hub tiles, six
game cards with the element shapes (section 100.1), friend rows, and PLAY / BACK. Hover is live on each board (point at
anything), and one card wears the blue focus ring.
- **R35A, Lift (recommended):** the control rises 3 px, its border turns white and a white rule grows under it. Enter
  takes 160 ms, eased out; exit takes 120 ms.
- **R35B, Trace:** one white light runs once around the cut border (420 ms), then the border rests one step brighter.
- **R35C, Sheen:** one soft band sweeps across the fill (520 ms), then the fill rests 8 % lighter.
- **R35D, Brackets:** the kit's corner brackets close in from outside (180 ms) and stay while the pointer is over.
- **R35Compare:** a button, a nav tab and a hub row at rest, hovered, focused and live, for all four options.
- **R35Notes:** the rules and the build notes.

**Rules every option keeps:**
- Hover is white. It is never the Selection blue (nothing is chosen yet), and elements have no colour.
- It plays once on enter and then rests; nothing idles.
- It is pointer-only. The pad and keys keep the sky-blue focus ring, and a control under both shows focus.
- Touch gets no hover, and locked controls answer nothing.

**Why A:** the rise and the rule say "this one" without a second colour, and it is cheap (one transform and one width).
B and D are the quiet choices where a control must not move (dense lists, the HUD). C looks richest but tells the
player least.

**Differs from the build:**
- One kit component, `KitHover`, would replace `KitHoverTint` on KitButton, KitTile, the game card and the nav tab.
- The look and its timings would live on the theme asset (`UIThemeSO`), so a pick changes every control at once.
- It animates only on enter and exit, so it costs nothing per frame while resting.

### 101.1 Hover C (Sheen), built (2026-10-28, awaiting in-editor approval)

**Picked (Shombith, 2026-10-28):** "I like C for the hover effects".

- **`KitHoverSheen`** (new, `Assets/_Scripts/UI/Kit/`): when the pointer enters a control, one soft band sweeps across
  it once (eased out, unscaled time), then the control rests.
  - It is built the first time a control is hovered: a `HoverSheen` child with a `RectMask2D` that layouts ignore, and
    a `Band` image inside it.
  - The band is switched off between sweeps, so a resting control costs nothing per frame.
  - Touch gets no sheen (`UIPointerType.Touch`), and the pad keeps the blue focus ring.
- **Where it plays:** `KitSelectableBase.OnPointerEnter` (every KitButton, KitTile, KitTextTab, KitBrandButton and the
  game cards) and `KitHoverTint.OnPointerEnter` (the nav tabs and keycaps). A locked tab's KitHoverTint is disabled,
  so it plays nothing.
- **The look lives on the theme:** `UIThemeSO` gains `hoverSheenSprite`, `hoverSheenSeconds` (0.52), `hoverSheenAlpha`
  (0.16) and `hoverSheenWidth` (0.45 of the control's width).
  - `UITheme.asset` is written by `Tools/Build/author_ui_lattice.py`, with a validation for the sprite and the timing.
  - Clearing `hoverSheenSprite` turns the sheen off everywhere.
- **The sprite:** `kit_sheen.png` (64 x 32 units, leaning about 15 degrees) comes from `Tools/Build/author_ui_kit_sprites.py`,
  with invariants (clear at both sides, solid on the centre line, leans the right way).
- The existing hover tints stay: KitButton's fill lift and KitHoverTint's word lift.

## 102. Rev 36 mockups: FTUE, redrawn - the captain arrives (2026-10-28; Shombith picked B)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-10-28):** "for the ftue I do not like them once bit. Up your game bhai", then "Leave that ref,
just make it good".

**What Rev 34 got wrong:**
- The screens behind her were empty seas with a placeholder line.
- She was a static cut-out stuck in a corner.
- The dim flattened the whole screen.
- In C, her line ran over the button she pointed at.

**On the canvas:** page "Rev 36 - FTUE, redrawn: the captain arrives"; the copies are
`Docs/MenuRedesignB/board/R36*.dc.html`.

Every step is a full game screen:
1. Home: the live cell, the nav with its icons, the pilot card, the four hub buttons.
2. Arcade: the window with six cards (the element shapes) and this week's game.
3. Configure: Skim Race, the Squirrel, intensity, pilots, START.
4. The first race: timer, goal, the four-hex ability row, boost.
5. The finish: 1st, 1:24.6, +120 crystals, RACE AGAIN.

On each step one control is lit in lime inside a hole in a 72 % dim. Settings and Skip guide (Y) stay live above it.
Trace enters from the side away from the control, and her panel never covers it. Each option board replays her
entrance per step.
- **R36A, Wingman (recommended):** her Squirrel streaks in along a skim trail that draws itself and ends on the
  control. Her comm opens beside the hull: hex portrait, name, the line typing at 45 characters a second, the prompt.
- **R36B, Comms:** a tall portrait window docks on the far edge. Her voice bars run while the line types, and a lime
  line runs to the control.
- **R36C, Cinematic:** the edge darkens, she leans in large, and her name (76 px), a rule, her line and the prompt
  are set beside her.
- **R36Compare:** the three options at rest on Home, Configure and the first race. **R36Notes:** the critique of
  Rev 34, the timings, the build.

**Picked (Shombith, 2026-10-29):** "Lets go with B" (Comms).

**Why A was recommended:** her vessel does the game's own move (a skim trail), and the trail is the pointer, so the eye follows it to
the control without an arrow. B suits in-match lines, since it covers least. C has the most character.

**Differs from the build:**
- The words go in the guide asset (MainQuest), one line per step, with the speaker from the pilot roster.
- The entrance, the trail and the spotlight hole are new view work on `MainMenuDialogueView`.
- Open (Shombith, Caleb): her voice, and her name.

### 102.1 Rev 36 B (Comms), built (2026-10-29, awaiting in-editor approval)

The captain's comms window rides on the existing guided path (`MenuGuide` + `MenuSpotlight`, the LOCKED rule: one lit
control, everything else dimmed and dead except Settings and Skip).
- **`GuideSpeakerSO`** (new, `Assets/_Scripts/UI/Guide/`) holds who speaks: name, title, eyebrow, portrait, the comms
  prefab, 45 characters a second, a 0.5 s dock. `GuideSpeaker_Trace.asset` is Trace with her roster portrait.
- **`GuideCommsView`** (new) behaves like the board:
  - it docks on the screen edge away from the lit control, and moves to the top when it would cover it;
  - the line types once the window has landed, the 18 voice bars move while it types, then the prompt (keycap and
    action) fades in;
  - a lime line runs from the window to the control.

  A new line replays the entrance. Every graphic is raycast-off, so the dim still decides what a press reaches.
- **`MenuSpotlight.Show`** gains a speaker overload. With a speaker, the comms window replaces the caption pill.
- **`QuestGuideToMicrogameNode`** gains `speaker`, one prompt per step (Arcade / Open / Fly) and `actionKeycap`.
  `MainQuest_Phase0_FirstLogin.asset` now speaks in Trace's voice.
- **Written by `Tools/Build/author_guide_comms.py`** (`--check`, `--self-test` with 5 plants): the prefab
  (`Assets/_Prefabs/UI Elements/Guide/GuideCommsView.prefab`), the speaker asset and the phase-asset fields.
- **Not covered yet:** the in-race and finish steps (4-5 on the board). The first-login guide stops at the Lesson; those
  steps need their own nodes.

## 103. Rev 37: connection states and rejoin (2026-10-29; approved by Shombith, built in 103.1)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-10-29):** "where are the others like the connections lost and reconnect ones?" and "If some one
leaves a game and then they boot up while the game is running the game should ask the user if they want to rejoin that
game".

**On the canvas:** page "Rev 37 - Connection: lost, reconnecting, back online, rejoin"; the copies are
`Docs/MenuRedesignB/board/R37*.dc.html`. This is a states round for the window built in section 100.2
(`DisconnectNoticeView`), which until now had no board.
- **R37Flow (interactive):** every state, picked from a strip.
- **R37Lost, R37Reconnecting, R37Online:** the three states that are already built. Words come from
  `DisconnectNoticeConfig`; Back Online closes itself after 2.5 s.
- **R37Match, "You dropped out" (new):** a guest whose link breaks is sent back to their own Home
  (`PartyInviteController.HandleHostLossAsync`) while the host hands their vessel to an AI pilot
  (`ServerPlayerVesselInitializer.ConvertPlayerToAI`). The game already does both; nothing tells the player. Offers
  REJOIN (lime, A) and NOT NOW (B).
- **R37HostLeft (new):** the host is a player, so the match ends with them. One line and BACK TO HOME; no rejoin.
- **R37Rejoin (new):** after sign-in, if the match you left is still running, Home opens with the mode's card, who
  is in it, "AI flying it now" on your seat, REJOIN and NOT NOW.
- **R37Notes:** the rules (lime only on the action that puts you back, nothing red) and the build.

**What the game does today** (read 2026-10-29; Block 6 "reconnection grace" in
`Docs/prompts/MULTIPLAYER_HARDENING_PROMPT.md`, not started):
- The party session is the match session: a UGS Multiplayer session with Relay, hosted by the party host.
- Nothing persists which match a player was in.
- Connection approval approves everyone and creates a new Player. A returning player would get a second vessel while
  the old one stays AI-flown, because `ConvertPlayerToAI` is one-way.
- `ReconnectService` takes an offline session back online by re-running the boot chain. It is not a match rejoin.

**What the rejoin build needs:**
1. Remember `{session id, mode}` locally while in a match as a guest; clear it on a normal end or leave.
2. On Home, ask presence whether a party mate still advertises that session in a match, and show R37Rejoin.
3. REJOIN joins by session id through the existing client-join path, with a rejoin token (the player's UGS id) in the
   approval payload, as spectators already do.
4. The host hands the AI-flown Player and vessel back to the returning client: ownership, `NetIsAI = false`, AI pilot
   off, client pairing re-run. It must not spawn a second one.

**Approved (Shombith, 2026-10-29):** "Go ahead with rev 37 I approve".

### 103.1 Rejoin, built (2026-10-29, awaiting a two-client test)

- **`MatchRejoin`** (new, `Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Multiplayer/`) is the shared piece:
  - the remembered match (PlayerPrefs `{session id, mode, saved, dropped}`, given up after 30 minutes);
  - the approval token `cosmicshore.rejoin.v1:<ugs id>`;
  - the host's pending rejoins;
  - `FindReclaimableSeat`: a spawned, AI-flown Player with the returning player's UGS id.
- **When the record is kept and dropped:**
  - Kept: a guest remembers the match when the host's config arrives in the game scene
    (`SyncGameConfigToClients_ClientRpc`). A drop (`HandleHostLossAsync`) marks it dropped and keeps it.
  - Forgotten: the match's end (`SyncGameEnd_ClientRpc`), a deliberate return (`SceneLoader.ReturnToMainMenu`),
    leaving the party, and NOT NOW / BACK TO HOME.
- **The window** is `DisconnectNotice`, the same style-B prefab. On Home it looks every 2 s for a party mate still
  advertising the remembered session in a match:
  - one found: it shows "You dropped out" (a drop under 3 minutes old) or "Rejoin {mode}?", with REJOIN and NOT NOW;
  - nobody for 20 s after a drop: "The host left", with BACK TO HOME;
  - nobody after a restart: the record is quietly forgotten.

  All the words are in `DisconnectNoticeConfig` (written by `author_disconnect_notice.py`).
- **REJOIN** calls `PartyInviteController.RejoinMatchAsync`: the same join as JOIN, with the rejoin token armed.
- **The host:** approval makes no Player for a rejoin with a seat to give back, and refuses one without. On connect, or
  on the client's roster request if that comes first, `TryReclaimSeat`:
  - returns ownership of the Player and vessel;
  - sets `NetIsAI` false and switches the AI pilot off;
  - tells that client (`SeatReclaimed_ClientRpc`), and sends the roster.
- **The client:** once ownership and the human flag land, it claims the Player as its own (`GameDataSO.ClaimLocalPlayer`,
  with no reset), re-initialises the pair (camera and input), frees input if the turn is running, and raises client
  ready.
- **Tests:** `MatchRejoinTests` (payload, record, expiry).
- **Not built, and the risks** (named for the two-client test):
  - no silent no-menu reconnect;
  - a crashed client may still hold its UGS membership, so the join fails "already a member";
  - re-initialising a vessel that is already initialised is new;
  - the game-scene guards for a client that joins from the menu mid-match are untested;
  - a host can never rejoin.
